<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Battle_of_Berbera">Battle of Berbera</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/British_attack_on_Berbera_(1827)" title="British attack on Berbera (1827)">British attack on Berbera (1827)</a></div>
<p>The first engagement between Somalis of the region and the British was in 1825 and ended violently.<sup id="cite_ref-d4IlR_100-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-d4IlR-100">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> This culminated in the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Berbera_1827" class="mw-redirect" title="Battle of Berbera 1827">Battle of Berbera</a> and a subsequent trade agreement between the <a href="/wiki/Habr_Awal" title="Habr Awal">Habr Awal</a> and the United Kingdom.<sup id="cite_ref-b6sTC_101-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-b6sTC-101">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-jFVbI_102-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-jFVbI-102">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> This was followed by a British treaty with the Governor of <a href="/wiki/Zeila" title="Zeila">Zeila</a> in 1840. An engagement was then started between the British and elders of <a href="/wiki/Garhajis" title="Garhajis">Habar Garhajis</a> and <a href="/wiki/Habr_Je%27lo" title="Habr Je&#39;lo">Habar Toljaala</a> clans of the <a href="/wiki/Isaaq" title="Isaaq">Isaaq</a> in 1855, followed a year later by the conclusion of the "Articles of Peace and Friendship" between the Habar Awal and <a href="/wiki/East_India_Company" title="East India Company">East India Company</a>. These engagements between the British and Somali clans culminated in the formal treaties the British signed with the henceforth 'British Somaliland' clans, which took place between 1884 and 1886 (treaties were signed with the Habar Awal, Gadabursi, Habar Toljaala, Habar Garhajis, Esa, and the Warsangali clans), this paved the way for the British to establish a <a href="/wiki/Protectorate" title="Protectorate">protectorate</a> in the region referred to as <a href="/wiki/British_Somaliland" title="British Somaliland">British Somaliland</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-vsQPx_103-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vsQPx-103">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> The British garrisoned the protectorate from <a href="/wiki/Aden_Settlement" class="mw-redirect" title="Aden Settlement">Aden</a> and administered it as part of <a href="/wiki/British_Indian_Empire" class="mw-redirect" title="British Indian Empire">British India</a> until 1898. British Somaliland was then administered by the <a href="/wiki/Foreign_Office" class="mw-redirect" title="Foreign Office">Foreign Office</a> until 1905, and afterwards by the <a href="/wiki/Colonial_Office" title="Colonial Office">Colonial Office</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-6ot4n_104-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6ot4n-104">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="British_Somaliland">British Somaliland</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Somaliland_campaign" title="Somaliland campaign">Somaliland campaign</a>, <a href="/wiki/Somaliland_campaign_(1920)" title="Somaliland campaign (1920)">Somaliland campaign (1920)</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Italian_conquest_of_British_Somaliland" class="mw-redirect" title="Italian conquest of British Somaliland">Italian conquest of British Somaliland</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Corriere_Della_Sera_-_17_agosto_1940_-_Offensiva_in_Africa_-_titolo.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/33/Corriere_Della_Sera_-_17_agosto_1940_-_Offensiva_in_Africa_-_titolo.JPG/170px-Corriere_Della_Sera_-_17_agosto_1940_-_Offensiva_in_Africa_-_titolo.JPG" decoding="async" width="170" height="128" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/33/Corriere_Della_Sera_-_17_agosto_1940_-_Offensiva_in_Africa_-_titolo.JPG/255px-Corriere_Della_Sera_-_17_agosto_1940_-_Offensiva_in_Africa_-_titolo.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/33/Corriere_Della_Sera_-_17_agosto_1940_-_Offensiva_in_Africa_-_titolo.JPG/340px-Corriere_Della_Sera_-_17_agosto_1940_-_Offensiva_in_Africa_-_titolo.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="1536" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Corriere_Della_Sera_-_17_agosto_1940_-_Offensiva_in_Africa_-_titolo.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><div class="center" style="width:auto; margin-left:auto; margin-right:auto;">The Italian newspaper <i><a href="/wiki/Corriere_della_Sera" title="Corriere della Sera">Corriere della Sera</a></i> covering the start of the <a href="/wiki/British_Somaliland" title="British Somaliland">British Somaliland</a> offensive</div></div></div></div>
<p>The Somaliland Campaign, also called the Anglo-Somali War or the Dervish War, was a series of military expeditions that took place between 1900 and 1920 in the <a href="/wiki/Horn_of_Africa" title="Horn of Africa">Horn of Africa</a>, pitting the <a href="/wiki/Dervish_state" class="mw-redirect" title="Dervish state">Dervishes</a> led by <a href="/wiki/Mohammed_Abdullah_Hassan" title="Mohammed Abdullah Hassan">Mohammed Abdullah Hassan</a> (nicknamed the "Mad Mullah") against the <a href="/wiki/British_Empire" title="British Empire">British</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-RdDPF_105-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RdDPF-105">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> The British were assisted in their offensives by the <a href="/wiki/Empire_of_Ethiopia" class="mw-redirect" title="Empire of Ethiopia">Ethiopians</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy" title="Kingdom of Italy">Italians</a>. During the <a href="/wiki/First_World_War" class="mw-redirect" title="First World War">First World War</a> (1914–1918), Hassan also received aid from the <a href="/wiki/Ottoman_Empire" title="Ottoman Empire">Ottomans</a>, <a href="/wiki/German_Empire" title="German Empire">Germans</a> and, for a time, from the Emperor <a href="/wiki/Iyasu_V_of_Ethiopia" class="mw-redirect" title="Iyasu V of Ethiopia">Iyasu V of Ethiopia</a>. The conflict ended when the British <a href="/wiki/Somaliland_campaign_(1920)" title="Somaliland campaign (1920)">aerially bombed</a> the Dervish capital of <a href="/wiki/Taleh" title="Taleh">Taleh</a> in February 1920.<sup id="cite_ref-zwS5A_106-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-zwS5A-106">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Fifth Expedition of the <a href="/wiki/Somaliland_campaign" title="Somaliland campaign">Somaliland campaign</a> in 1920 was the final <a href="/wiki/British_Empire" title="British Empire">British</a> expedition against the <a href="/wiki/Dervish_State" class="mw-redirect" title="Dervish State">Dervish</a> forces of <a href="/wiki/Mohammed_Abdullah_Hassan" title="Mohammed Abdullah Hassan">Mohammed Abdullah Hassan</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Somali_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Somali people">Somali</a> religious leader. Although most of the combat took place in January of the year, British troops had begun preparations for the assault as early as November 1919. The British forces included elements of the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Air_Force" title="Royal Air Force">Royal Air Force</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Somaliland_Camel_Corps" title="Somaliland Camel Corps">Somaliland Camel Corps</a>. After three weeks of battle, Hassan's Dervishes were defeated, bringing an effective end to their 20-year resistance.<sup id="cite_ref-Baker_107-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Baker-107">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Italian conquest of British Somaliland was a military campaign in East Africa, which took place in August 1940 between forces of <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy" title="Kingdom of Italy">Italy</a> and those of several British and <a href="/wiki/British_Commonwealth" class="mw-redirect" title="British Commonwealth">Commonwealth</a> countries. The Italian expedition was part of the <a href="/wiki/East_African_Campaign_(World_War_II)" class="mw-redirect" title="East African Campaign (World War II)">East African Campaign</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-hG8Mf_108-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hG8Mf-108">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Anti-colonial_resistance">Anti-colonial resistance</span></h3>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Burao_Tax_Revolt_and_RAF_bombing">Burao Tax Revolt and RAF bombing</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/1922_Burao_Tax_Revolt" class="mw-redirect" title="1922 Burao Tax Revolt">1922 Burao Tax Revolt</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:99px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Captainallangibb.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/e/ee/Captainallangibb.jpg" decoding="async" width="97" height="200" class="thumbimage" data-file-width="97" data-file-height="200" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Captainallangibb.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Captain Allan Gibb</div></div></div>
<p>The people of Burao clashed with the British in 1922 after a heavy tax was imposed upon them. They revolted in opposition to the tax and this caused them to riot and attack British government officials. In the ensuing disturbances a shootout between the British and Burao residents broke out, Captain Allan Gibb, a Dervish war veteran and district commissioner, was shot and killed. The British fearing they could not contain the revolt requested from Sir <a href="/wiki/Winston_Churchill" title="Winston Churchill">Winston Churchill</a>, then <a href="/wiki/Secretary_of_State_for_the_Colonies" title="Secretary of State for the Colonies">Secretary of State for the Colonies</a>, to send troops from <a href="/wiki/Aden" title="Aden">Aden</a> and Airplane bombers in order to bomb Burao and livestock of the revolting clans to quell any further rebellion.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> The RAF planes arrived at Burao within two days and proceeded to bomb the town with incendiaries, effectively burning the entire settlement to the ground.<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-corr_1922_113-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-corr_1922-113">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Telegram from <a href="/wiki/Geoffrey_Archer_(colonial_administrator)" title="Geoffrey Archer (colonial administrator)">Sir Geoffrey Archer</a>, Governor of British Somaliland to <a href="/wiki/Sir_Winston_Churchill" class="mw-redirect" title="Sir Winston Churchill">Sir Winston Churchill</a> the <a href="/wiki/Secretary_of_State_for_the_Colonies" title="Secretary of State for the Colonies">Secretary of State for the Colonies</a>:
</p>
<blockquote><p>I deeply regret to inform that during an affray at Burao yesterday between Rer Sugulleh and Akils of other tribes Captain Gibb was shot dead. Having called out Camel corps company to quell the disturbance, he went forward himself with his interpreter, whereupon fire opened on him by some Rer segulleh riflemen and he was instantly killed..Miscreants then disappeared under the cover of darkness.
In order to meet the situation created by the Murder of Gibb, we require two aeroplanes for about fourteen days. I have arranged with resident, Aden, for these. And made formal application, which please confirm. It is proposed they fly via Perim, confining sea crossing to 12 miles. We propose to inflict fine of 2,500 camels on implicated sections, who are practically isolated and demand surrender of man who killed Gibbs. He is known. Fine to be doubled in failure to comply with latter conditions and aeroplanes to be used to bomb stock on grazing grounds.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p><a href="/wiki/Sir_Winston_Churchill" class="mw-redirect" title="Sir Winston Churchill">Sir Winston Churchill</a> reporting on the Burao incident at the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Commons" title="House of Commons">House of Commons</a>:
</p>
<blockquote><p>On 25th February the Governor of Somaliland telegraphed that an affray between tribesmen had taken place at Burao on the previous day, in the course of which Captain Allan Gibb, D.S.O., D.C.M., the District Commissioner at Burao, had been shot dead. Captain Gibb had advanced with his interpreter to quell the disturbance, when 1954 fire was opened upon him by some riflemen, and he was instantly killed. The murderers escaped under cover of falling darkness.
Captain Gibb was an officer of long and valued service in Somaliland, whose loss I deeply regret. From the information available, his murder does not appear to have been premeditated, but it inevitably had a disturbing effect upon the surrounding tribes, and immediate dispositions of troops became necessary in order to ensure the apprehension and punishment of those responsible for the murder. On 27th February the Governor telegraphed that, in order to meet the situation which had arisen, he required two aeroplanes for purposes of demonstration, and suggested that two aeroplanes from the Royal Air Force Detachment at Aden should fly over to Berber a from Aden. He also telegraphed that in certain circumstances it might become necessary to ask for reinforcements of troops to be sent to the Protectorate.<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>James Lawrence author of <i>Imperial Rearguard: Wars of Empire</i> writes
</p>
<blockquote><p>[Gibb]..was murdered by rioters during a protest against taxation at Burao. Governor Archer immediately called for aircraft which were at Burao within two days. The inhabitants of the native township were turned out of their houses, and the entire area was razed by a combination of bombing, machine-gun fire and burning.<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>After the RAF aircraft bombed Burao to the ground, the leaders of the rebellion acquiesced, agreeing to pay a fine for Gibbs death, but they refused to identify and apprehend the accused individuals. Most of the men responsible for Gibb's shooting evaded capture. In light of the failure to implement the taxation without provoking a violent response, the British abandoned the policy altogether.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-corr_1922_113-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-corr_1922-113">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="1945_Sheikh_Bashir_Rebellion">1945 Sheikh Bashir Rebellion</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/1945_Sheikh_Bashir_Rebellion" class="mw-redirect" title="1945 Sheikh Bashir Rebellion">1945 Sheikh Bashir Rebellion</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:302px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Sheikh_Bashir_praying.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Sheikh_Bashir_praying.jpg/300px-Sheikh_Bashir_praying.jpg" decoding="async" width="300" height="186" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Sheikh_Bashir_praying.jpg/450px-Sheikh_Bashir_praying.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Sheikh_Bashir_praying.jpg/600px-Sheikh_Bashir_praying.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="495" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Sheikh_Bashir_praying.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Sheikh Bashir praying <a href="/wiki/Sunnah_prayer" title="Sunnah prayer">Sunnah prayer</a>, 1920</div></div></div>
<p>The 1945 Sheikh Bashir Rebellion was a rebellion waged by tribesmen of the <a href="/wiki/Habr_Je%27lo" title="Habr Je&#39;lo">Habr Je'lo</a> clan in the former <a href="/wiki/British_Somaliland" title="British Somaliland">British Somaliland</a> protectorate against British authorities in July 1945 led by <a href="/wiki/Sheikh_Bashir" title="Sheikh Bashir">Sheikh Bashir</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Somalis" title="Somalis">Somali</a> religious leader.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_119-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-119">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 2 July, Sheikh Bashir collected 25 of his followers in the town of <a href="/wiki/Wadamago" title="Wadamago">Wadamago</a> and transported them on a lorry to the vicinity of <a href="/wiki/Burao" title="Burao">Burao</a>, where he distributed arms to half of his followers. On the evening of 3 July the group entered Burao and opened fire on the police guard of the central prison in the city, which was filled with prisoners arrested for previous demonstrations. The group also attacked the house of the district commissioner of <a href="/wiki/Burao_District" title="Burao District">Burao District</a>, Major Chambers, resulting in the death of Major Chamber's police guard before escaping to Bur Dhab, a strategic mountain south-east of Burao, where Sheikh Bashir's small unit occupied a fort and took up a defensive position in anticipation of a British counterattack.<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The British campaign against Sheikh Bashir's troops proved abortive after several defeats as his forces kept moving from place to place and avoiding any permanent location. No sooner had the expedition left the area, than the news traveled fast among the Somali nomads across the plain. The war had exposed the British administration to humiliation. The government came to a conclusion that another expedition against him would be useless; that they must build a railway, make roads and effectively occupy the whole of the protectorate, or else abandon the interior completely. The latter course was decided upon, and during the first months of 1945, the advance posts were withdrawn and the British administration confined to the coast town of <a href="/wiki/Berbera" title="Berbera">Berbera</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sheikh Bashir settled many disputes among the tribes in the vicinity, which kept them from raiding each other. He was generally thought to settle disputes through the use of Islamic <a href="/wiki/Sharia" title="Sharia">Sharia</a> and gathered around him a strong following.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_122-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-122">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The British administration recruited <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">Indian</a> and <a href="/wiki/South_Africa" title="South Africa">South African</a> troops, led by police general James David, to fight against Sheikh Bashir and had intelligence plans to capture him alive. The British authorities mobilized a police force, and eventually on 7 July found Sheikh Bashir and his unit in defensive positions behind their fortifications in the mountains of Bur Dhab. After clashes Sheikh Bashir and his second-in-command, Alin Yusuf Ali, nicknamed Qaybdiid, were killed. A third rebel was wounded and was captured along with two other rebels. The rest fled the fortifications and dispersed. On the British side the police general leading the British troops as well as a number of Indian and South African troops perished in the clashes, and a policeman was injured.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_122-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-122">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After his death, Sheikh Bashir was widely hailed by locals as a martyr and was held in great reverence. His family took quick action to remove his body from the place of his death at Geela-eeg mountain, about 20 miles from <a href="/wiki/Burao" title="Burao">Burao</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="State_of_Somaliland_.28Independence.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="State_of_Somaliland_(Independence)">State of Somaliland (Independence)</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/State_of_Somaliland" title="State of Somaliland">State of Somaliland</a> and <a href="/wiki/Independence_Day_(State_of_Somaliland)" title="Independence Day (State of Somaliland)">Independence Day (State of Somaliland)</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Somaliland_Flying_for_the_first_time_The_White_and_Blue_Somali_Flag_at_the_Independence_Celebrations_on_26_June_1960.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0b/Somaliland_Flying_for_the_first_time_The_White_and_Blue_Somali_Flag_at_the_Independence_Celebrations_on_26_June_1960.jpg/170px-Somaliland_Flying_for_the_first_time_The_White_and_Blue_Somali_Flag_at_the_Independence_Celebrations_on_26_June_1960.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="201" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0b/Somaliland_Flying_for_the_first_time_The_White_and_Blue_Somali_Flag_at_the_Independence_Celebrations_on_26_June_1960.jpg/255px-Somaliland_Flying_for_the_first_time_The_White_and_Blue_Somali_Flag_at_the_Independence_Celebrations_on_26_June_1960.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0b/Somaliland_Flying_for_the_first_time_The_White_and_Blue_Somali_Flag_at_the_Independence_Celebrations_on_26_June_1960.jpg/340px-Somaliland_Flying_for_the_first_time_The_White_and_Blue_Somali_Flag_at_the_Independence_Celebrations_on_26_June_1960.jpg 2x" data-file-width="426" data-file-height="503" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Somaliland_Flying_for_the_first_time_The_White_and_Blue_Somali_Flag_at_the_Independence_Celebrations_on_26_June_1960.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The White and Blue Somali Flag at the <a href="/wiki/Independence_Day_(State_of_Somaliland)" title="Independence Day (State of Somaliland)">Independence</a> celebrations on 26 June 1960 when the prime minister of the <a href="/wiki/State_of_Somaliland" title="State of Somaliland">State of Somaliland</a> and the <a href="/wiki/President_of_Somaliland" title="President of Somaliland">second president of Somaliland</a>, <a href="/wiki/Muhammad_Haji_Ibrahim_Egal" title="Muhammad Haji Ibrahim Egal">Muhammad Haji Ibrahim Egal</a>, salute the flag.</div></div></div>
<p>In May 1960, the British government stated that it would be prepared to grant independence to the then <a href="/wiki/Protectorate" title="Protectorate">protectorate</a> of British Somaliland, with the intention that the territory would unite with the <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italian</a>-administered <a href="/wiki/Trust_Territory_of_Somaliland" title="Trust Territory of Somaliland">Trust Territory of Somaliland under Italian Administration</a> (the former <a href="/wiki/Italian_Somaliland" title="Italian Somaliland">Italian Somaliland</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-0JY3U_124-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-0JY3U-124">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> The Legislative Council of British Somaliland passed a resolution in April 1960 requesting independence and union with the Trust Territory of Somaliland, which was scheduled to gain independence on 1 July that year. The legislative councils of both territories agreed to this proposal following a joint conference in <a href="/wiki/Mogadishu" title="Mogadishu">Mogadishu</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-wardheernews.com_125-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wardheernews.com-125">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup>
On 26 June 1960, the former British Somaliland protectorate briefly obtained independence as the State of Somaliland, with the Trust Territory of Somaliland following suit five days later.<sup id="cite_ref-Encyclopædia_Britannica_2002_p.835_18-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encyclopædia_Britannica_2002_p.835-18">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> During its brief period of independence, the <a href="/wiki/State_of_Somaliland" title="State of Somaliland">State of Somaliland</a> garnered recognition from thirty-five sovereign states.<sup id="cite_ref-Kaplan257_126-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kaplan257-126">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> However, the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a> merely acknowledged Somaliland's independence:
</p>
<blockquote><p>The United States did not extend formal recognition to Somaliland, but Secretary of State Herter sent a congratulatory message dated June 26 to the Somaliland Council of Ministers.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>The following day, on 27 June 1960, the newly convened Somaliland Legislative Assembly approved a bill that would formally allow for the union of the State of Somaliland with the Trust Territory of Somaliland on 1 July 1960.<sup id="cite_ref-wardheernews.com_125-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wardheernews.com-125">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Somali_Republic_.28union_with_Somalia.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Somali_Republic_(union_with_Somalia)">Somali Republic (union with Somalia)</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Somali_Republic" title="Somali Republic">Somali Republic</a> and <a href="/wiki/Greater_Somalia" title="Greater Somalia">Greater Somalia</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/1961_revolt_in_Somalia" title="1961 revolt in Somalia">1961 revolt in Somalia</a></div>
<p>On 1 July 1960, the <a href="/wiki/State_of_Somaliland" title="State of Somaliland">State of Somaliland</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Trust_Territory_of_Somaliland" title="Trust Territory of Somaliland">Trust Territory of Somaliland</a> (the former <a href="/wiki/Italian_Somaliland" title="Italian Somaliland">Italian Somaliland</a>) united as planned to form the <a href="/wiki/Somalia" title="Somalia">Somali Republic</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-buluugleey.com_128-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-buluugleey.com-128">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Tmotss_129-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Tmotss-129">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> Inspired by <a href="/wiki/Somali_nationalism" title="Somali nationalism">Somali nationalism</a>, the northerners were initially enthusiastic about the union.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERichards201484–85_41-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERichards201484–85-41">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> A government was formed by <a href="/wiki/Abdullahi_Issa" title="Abdullahi Issa">Abdullahi Issa</a>, with <a href="/wiki/Aden_Abdullah_Osman_Daar" class="mw-redirect" title="Aden Abdullah Osman Daar">Aden Abdullah Osman Daar</a> as <a href="/wiki/List_of_Presidents_of_Somalia" class="mw-redirect" title="List of Presidents of Somalia">President</a> and <a href="/wiki/Abdirashid_Ali_Shermarke" class="mw-redirect" title="Abdirashid Ali Shermarke">Abdirashid Ali Shermarke</a> as <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Somalia" class="mw-redirect" title="Prime Minister of Somalia">Prime Minister</a> (later to become president, from 1967 to 1969). On 20 July 1961 and through a popular <a href="/wiki/Referendum" title="Referendum">referendum</a>, the Somali people ratified a new constitution, which was first drafted in 1960.<sup id="cite_ref-y4Fnm_130-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-y4Fnm-130">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> The constitution had little support in the former Somaliland, and was believed to favour the south. Many northerners boycotted the referendum in protest, and over 60% of those who voted in the north were against the new constitution. Regardless, the referendum passed, and Somaliland became quickly dominated by southerners. As result, dissatisfaction became widespread in the north, and support for the union plummeted. British-trained Somaliland officers attempted a <a href="/wiki/1961_revolt_in_Somalia" title="1961 revolt in Somalia">revolt to end the union in December 1961</a>. Their uprising failed, and Somaliland continued to be marginalized by the south during the next decades.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERichards201484–85_41-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERichards201484–85-41">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1967, <a href="/wiki/Muhammad_Haji_Ibrahim_Egal" title="Muhammad Haji Ibrahim Egal">Muhammad Haji Ibrahim Egal</a> became Prime Minister, a position to which he was appointed by Shermarke. Shermarke was assassinated two years later by one of his own bodyguards. His murder was quickly followed by a military <a href="/wiki/Coup_d%27%C3%A9tat" title="Coup d&#39;état">coup d'état</a> on 21 October 1969 (the day after his funeral), in which the <a href="/wiki/Military_of_Somalia" class="mw-redirect" title="Military of Somalia">Somalian Army</a> seized power without encountering armed opposition. The putsch was spearheaded by Major General <a href="/wiki/Siad_Barre" title="Siad Barre">Mohamed Siad Barre</a>, who at the time commanded the army.<sup id="cite_ref-Myswenvwp_131-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Myswenvwp-131">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> The new regime would go on to rule Somalia for the next 22 years.<sup id="cite_ref-Mr87M_132-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mr87M-132">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Somali_National_Movement.2C_Barre_persecution"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Somali_National_Movement,_Barre_persecution">Somali National Movement, Barre persecution</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Somali_National_Movement" title="Somali National Movement">Somali National Movement</a>, <a href="/wiki/Isaaq_genocide" title="Isaaq genocide">Isaaq genocide</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Somaliland_War_of_Independence" title="Somaliland War of Independence">Somaliland War of Independence</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:252px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Somaliland,_fighters_of_the_Somali_National_Movement_(SNM),_1980s.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Somaliland%2C_fighters_of_the_Somali_National_Movement_%28SNM%29%2C_1980s.jpg/250px-Somaliland%2C_fighters_of_the_Somali_National_Movement_%28SNM%29%2C_1980s.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="164" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Somaliland%2C_fighters_of_the_Somali_National_Movement_%28SNM%29%2C_1980s.jpg/375px-Somaliland%2C_fighters_of_the_Somali_National_Movement_%28SNM%29%2C_1980s.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Somaliland%2C_fighters_of_the_Somali_National_Movement_%28SNM%29%2C_1980s.jpg/500px-Somaliland%2C_fighters_of_the_Somali_National_Movement_%28SNM%29%2C_1980s.jpg 2x" data-file-width="960" data-file-height="630" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Somaliland,_fighters_of_the_Somali_National_Movement_(SNM),_1980s.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>SNM fighters, late 1980s</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Hargeisa_destroyed_by_Somali_government.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/Hargeisa_destroyed_by_Somali_government.jpg/200px-Hargeisa_destroyed_by_Somali_government.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="136" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/Hargeisa_destroyed_by_Somali_government.jpg/300px-Hargeisa_destroyed_by_Somali_government.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/Hargeisa_destroyed_by_Somali_government.jpg/400px-Hargeisa_destroyed_by_Somali_government.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1744" data-file-height="1183" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Hargeisa_destroyed_by_Somali_government.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Up to 90% of <a href="/wiki/Hargeisa" title="Hargeisa">Hargeisa</a> (2nd largest city of the <a href="/wiki/Somali_Republic" title="Somali Republic">Somali Republic</a>) was destroyed by the <a href="/wiki/Somali_government" class="mw-redirect" title="Somali government">Somali government</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Moral_authority" title="Moral authority">moral authority</a> of Barre's government was gradually eroded, as many Somalis became disillusioned with life under military rule. By the mid-1980s, resistance movements supported by Ethiopia's communist <a href="/wiki/Derg" title="Derg">Derg</a> administration had sprung up across the country, which lead to the <a href="/wiki/Somaliland_War_of_Independence" title="Somaliland War of Independence">Somaliland War of Independence</a>. Barre responded by ordering punitive measures against those he perceived as locally supporting the guerrillas, especially in the northern regions. The clampdown included bombing of cities, with the northwestern administrative centre of <a href="/wiki/Hargeisa" title="Hargeisa">Hargeisa</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Somali_National_Movement" title="Somali National Movement">Somali National Movement</a> (SNM) stronghold, among the targeted areas in 1988.<sup id="cite_ref-Locsg_46-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Locsg-46">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-IYEz3_133-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IYEz3-133">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> The bombardment was led by General <a href="/wiki/Mohammed_Said_Hersi_Morgan" title="Mohammed Said Hersi Morgan">Mohammed Said Hersi Morgan</a>, Barre's son-in-law.<sup id="cite_ref-UKPZE_134-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UKPZE-134">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In May 1988, the SNM launched a <a href="/wiki/1988_Hargeisa-Burao_offensive" title="1988 Hargeisa-Burao offensive">major offensive</a> on the cities of <a href="/wiki/Hargeisa" title="Hargeisa">Hargeisa</a> and <a href="/wiki/Burao" title="Burao">Burao</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:04_136-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:04-136">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> then the second and third largest cities of <a href="/wiki/Somalia" title="Somalia">Somalia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:21_139-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:21-139">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> The SNM captured Burao on 27 May within two hours,<sup id="cite_ref-:12_140-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:12-140">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> while the SNM entered Hargeisa on 29 May, overrunning most of the city apart from its airport by 1 June.<sup id="cite_ref-:04_136-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:04-136">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to Abou Jeng and other scholars, the Barre regime rule was marked by a targeted brutal persecution of the <a href="/wiki/Isaaq" title="Isaaq">Isaaq</a> clan.<sup id="cite_ref-bzPKm_141-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bzPKm-141">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-DeSRJ_142-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DeSRJ-142">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> Mohamed Haji Ingiriis and <a href="/wiki/Chris_Mullin_(politician)" title="Chris Mullin (politician)">Chris Mullin</a> state that the clampdown by the Barre regime against the Hargeisa-based Somali National Movement targeted the Isaaq clan, to which most members of the SNM belonged. They refer to the clampdown as the <a href="/wiki/Isaaq_Genocide" class="mw-redirect" title="Isaaq Genocide">Isaaq Genocide</a> or Hargeisa Holocaust.<sup id="cite_ref-TQ11H_143-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TQ11H-143">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-0CBv8_144-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-0CBv8-144">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>&#160;A <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> investigation concluded that the crime of genocide was "conceived, planned and perpetrated by the Somali Government against the Isaaq people".<sup id="cite_ref-Mburu_145-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mburu-145">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> The number of civilian casualties is estimated to be between 50,000 and 100,000 according to various sources,<sup id="cite_ref-Peifer_146-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Peifer-146">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-taQH4_147-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-taQH4-147">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Jones_148-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Jones-148">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> while some reports estimate the total civilian deaths to be upwards of 200,000 Isaaq civilians.<sup id="cite_ref-eeDVy_149-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-eeDVy-149">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup> Along with the deaths, Barre regime bombarded and razed the second and third largest cities in Somalia, <a href="/wiki/Hargeisa" title="Hargeisa">Hargeisa</a> and <a href="/wiki/Burao" title="Burao">Burao</a>, respectively.<sup id="cite_ref-Eaf0N_150-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Eaf0N-150">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> This displaced an estimated 400,000 local residents to <a href="/wiki/Hart_Sheik" title="Hart Sheik">Hart Sheik</a> in Ethiopia;<sup id="cite_ref-BXq6o_151-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BXq6o-151">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-LbREj_152-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LbREj-152">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-4ZCBy_153-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4ZCBy-153">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup> another 400,000 individuals were also internally displaced.<sup id="cite_ref-sh6vy_154-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-sh6vy-154">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-chsTS_155-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-chsTS-155">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-LQiHc_156-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LQiHc-156">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The counterinsurgency by the Barre regime against the SNM targeted the rebel group's civilian base of support, escalating into a genocidal onslaught against the Isaaq clan. This led to anarchy and violent campaigns by fragmented militias, which then wrested power at a local level.<sup id="cite_ref-yEPtQ_157-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-yEPtQ-157">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup> The Barre regime's persecution was not limited to the Isaaq, as it targeted other clans such as the <a href="/wiki/Hawiye" title="Hawiye">Hawiye</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-hWrRd_158-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hWrRd-158">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Richards2016p98_159-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Richards2016p98-159">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> The Barre regime collapsed in January 1991. Thereafter, as the political situation in Somaliland stabilized, the displaced people returned to their homes, the militias were demobilized or incorporated into the army, and tens of thousands of houses and businesses were reconstructed from rubble.<sup id="cite_ref-oksO0_160-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-oksO0-160">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Restoration_of_sovereignty_.28end_of_the_unity_with_Somalia.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Restoration_of_sovereignty_(end_of_the_unity_with_Somalia)">Restoration of sovereignty (end of the unity with Somalia)</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Somaliland_Peace_Process" title="Somaliland Peace Process">Somaliland Peace Process</a> and <a href="/wiki/Somaliland_Declaration_of_Independence" title="Somaliland Declaration of Independence">Somaliland Declaration of Independence</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Hargeisa_War_Memorial_2012.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/03/Hargeisa_War_Memorial_2012.jpg/170px-Hargeisa_War_Memorial_2012.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="113" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/03/Hargeisa_War_Memorial_2012.jpg/255px-Hargeisa_War_Memorial_2012.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/03/Hargeisa_War_Memorial_2012.jpg/340px-Hargeisa_War_Memorial_2012.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5184" data-file-height="3456" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Hargeisa_War_Memorial_2012.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Hargeisa_War_Memorial" title="Hargeisa War Memorial">MiG monument in Hargeisa</a> commemorating Somaliland's breakaway from the rest of Somalia in 1991</div></div></div>
<p>Although the SNM at its inception had a unionist constitution, it eventually began to pursue independence, looking to secede from the rest of Somalia.<sup id="cite_ref-Sqfirhbmsscf_161-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sqfirhbmsscf-161">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> Under the leadership of <a href="/wiki/Abdirahman_Ahmed_Ali_Tuur" title="Abdirahman Ahmed Ali Tuur">Abdirahman Ahmed Ali Tuur</a>, the local administration declared the northwestern Somali territories independent at a conference held in <a href="/wiki/Burao" title="Burao">Burao</a> between 27 April 1991 and 15 May 1991.<sup id="cite_ref-boAu1_162-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-boAu1-162">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> Tuur then became the newly established Somaliland polity's first President, but subsequently renounced the separatist platform in 1994 and began instead to publicly seek and advocate reconciliation with the rest of Somalia under a power-sharing <a href="/wiki/Federalism" title="Federalism">federal</a> system of governance.<sup id="cite_ref-Sqfirhbmsscf_161-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sqfirhbmsscf-161">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Muhammad_Haji_Ibrahim_Egal" title="Muhammad Haji Ibrahim Egal">Muhammad Haji Ibrahim Egal</a> was appointed as Tuur's successor in 1993 by the Grand Conference of National Reconciliation in <a href="/wiki/Borama" title="Borama">Borama</a>, which met for four months, leading to a gradual improvement in security, as well as a consolidation of the new territory.<sup id="cite_ref-wRrOl_163-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wRrOl-163">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup> Egal was reappointed in 1997, and remained in power until his death on 3 May 2002. The vice-president, <a href="/wiki/Dahir_Riyale_Kahin" title="Dahir Riyale Kahin">Dahir Riyale Kahin</a>, who was during the 1980s the highest-ranking <a href="/wiki/National_Security_Service_(Somalia)" title="National Security Service (Somalia)">National Security Service</a> (NSS) officer in <a href="/wiki/Berbera" title="Berbera">Berbera</a> in Siad Barre's government, was sworn in as president shortly afterward.<sup id="cite_ref-Albla_164-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Albla-164">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup> In 2003, Kahin became the first elected president of Somaliland.<sup id="cite_ref-3qgvQ_165-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3qgvQ-165">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/War_in_Somalia_(2006%E2%80%93present)" class="mw-redirect" title="War in Somalia (2006–present)">war in southern Somalia</a> between <a href="/wiki/Al-Shabaab_(militant_group)" title="Al-Shabaab (militant group)">Islamist insurgents</a> on the one hand, and the <a href="/wiki/Federal_Government_of_Somalia" title="Federal Government of Somalia">Federal Government of Somalia</a> and its <a href="/wiki/African_Union" title="African Union">African Union</a> allies on the other, has for the most part not directly affected Somaliland, which, like neighbouring <a href="/wiki/Puntland" title="Puntland">Puntland</a>, has remained relatively stable.<sup id="cite_ref-HUSwk_166-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HUSwk-166">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-TAE8S_167-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TAE8S-167">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="2001_constitutional_referendum">2001 constitutional referendum</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/2001_Somaliland_constitutional_referendum" title="2001 Somaliland constitutional referendum">2001 Somaliland constitutional referendum</a></div>
<p>In August 2000, <a href="/wiki/Muhammad_Haji_Ibrahim_Egal" title="Muhammad Haji Ibrahim Egal">President Egal's government</a> distributed thousands of copies of the proposed constitution throughout Somaliland for consideration and review by the people. One critical clause of the 130 individual articles of the constitution would ratify Somaliland's self-declared independence and final separation from <a href="/wiki/Somalia" title="Somalia">Somalia</a>, restoring the nation's independence for the first time since 1960. In late March 2001, <a href="/wiki/Muhammad_Haji_Ibrahim_Egal" title="Muhammad Haji Ibrahim Egal">President Egal</a> set the date for the referendum on the Constitution for 31 May 2001.<sup id="cite_ref-1IyKy_168-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1IyKy-168">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A constitutional referendum was held in Somaliland on 31 May 2001.<sup id="cite_ref-zxHzV_169-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-zxHzV-169">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> The referendum was held on a draft <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Somaliland" title="Constitution of Somaliland">constitution</a> that affirmed Somaliland's independence from <a href="/wiki/Somalia" title="Somalia">Somalia</a>. 99.9% of eligible voters took part in the referendum and 97.1% of them voted in favour of the constitution.<sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics_and_government">Politics and government</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Somaliland" title="Government of Somaliland">Government of Somaliland</a> and <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Somaliland" title="Politics of Somaliland">Politics of Somaliland</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/List_of_Somaliland_politicians" title="List of Somaliland politicians">List of Somaliland politicians</a></div>
